In the semantics, we consider two subterms equal if they agree on all (nested) markings.

Definition 30

(Semantics of extended local types)
.

Let
\mathit{EL}
be an extended local type for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
We construct a state machine
\mathsf{EAut}(\mathit{EL})=(Q,\Sigma,δ,q₀,F)
with

•

Q
is the set of all syntactic subterms in
\mathit{EL}
,

Σ=\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
,

q₀=\mathit{EL}
,

•

F=\{[\mathit{EL}^{\prime},\ast]\mid[EL^{\prime},\ast]
syntactic subterm of
EL\}
, and

•

δ
is the smallest set containing

–

([\IntCh_{i∈I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!m_{i}.[\mathit{EL}_{i},\circledast_{i}],\circledast^{\prime}],{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!m_{i},[EL_{i},\circledast_{i}])
for every
i\in I
where

\circledast^{\prime}\in\{\ast,\circ\}
and

\circledast_{i}\in\{\ast,\circ\}
for each
i∈I
,

–

([\ExtCh_{i∈I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}?m_{i}.[\mathit{EL}_{i},\circledast_{i}],\circledast^{\prime}],{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}?m_{i},[EL_{i},\circledast_{i}])
for every
i\in I
where

\circledast^{\prime}\in\{\ast,\circ\}
and

\circledast_{i}\in\{\ast,\circ\}
for each
i∈I
, as well as

–

([μt.[\mathit{EL}^{\prime},\circledast^{\prime}],\circledast^{\prime\prime}],ε,[EL,\circledast^{\prime}])
and
([t,\circledast_{t}],ε,[μt.[EL^{\prime},\circledast^{\prime}],\circledast^{\prime\prime}])

for each subterm
[\mu t.[EL^{\prime},\circledast^{\prime}],\circledast^{\prime\prime}]
and
[t,\circledast_{t}]
of
EL
with
\circledast_{t},\circledast^{\prime},\circledast^{\prime\prime}\in\{\ast,\circ\}
.

We define the language of
\mathit{EL}
as language of this state machine:

\mathcal{L}(\mathit{EL})=\mathcal{L}(\mathsf{EAut}(EL))
.

It is straightforward to turn an extended (availability annotated) local type into a (availability annotated) local type by recursively projecting onto the first component of the extended local type and hence ignoring the markers
\ast
and
\circ
.
Therefore, we apply this implicit coercion for operations defined on (availability annotated) local types only.

(a)

To be Unfolded

(b)

Unfolded until all roles are blocked

Figure 4
:
An HMSC and some Unfolding

Example 31
.

Consider the HMSC in
Fig.

4(a)
and its unfolding in
Fig.

4(b)
.
The unfolding exemplifies that one needs to unfold the recursion three times until all roles get blocked.
Still, if there were two more roles that would send messages to each other independently, one needs to account for these messages with a recursion.

The set of blocked roles does only increase.
If some message is blocked, two roles are blocked from the beginning and one iteration of the recursion is always present in any type.
In this case, we might need to unfold a recursion up to
\lvert\mathcal{P}\rvert-3
times to reach a fixed point of blocked roles.
In general, a fixed point is reached after
\lvert\mathcal{P}\rvert-2
unfoldings.

Definition 32

(Blocked projection operator)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be some global projectable global type,
E
a set of recursion variables,
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
a set of roles and
n
some natural number.
Let
T
be a set of recursion variables and
\eta\colon T\to\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{P})\times\mathbb{N}
.
We define the blocked projection
{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}
of
\mathbf{G}
parametrised by
n
onto a role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
inductively:

0{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}\coloneq[0{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},\ast]

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mu t.G^{\prime}}}\right){\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}\coloneq\begin{cases}[\langle μt.(G{\upharpoonright}^{E∪\{t\}}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}),\operatorname{avail}(\{R\},\{t\},G)\rangle,\circ]\text{ and $\eta(t)\coloneq(\mathcal{B},0)$}\\
\hfill\text{if }G{\upharpoonright}^{E∪\{t\}}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq[\langle t,\_\rangle,\_]∧t\text{ occurs in }G{\upharpoonright}^{E∪\{t\}}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\\
\langle(G{\upharpoonright}^{E∪\{t\}}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}),\operatorname{avail}(\{R\},\{t\},G)\rangle\hskip 16.38895pt\text{if }t\text{ does not occur in }G{\upharpoonright}^{E∪\{t\}}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\\
[\langle 0,∅\rangle,\ast]\hfill\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

t{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}\coloneq\begin{cases}[\langle\mu t^{\prime}.((\mathit{get\mu}_{\mathbf{G}}(t))[t^{\prime}/t]){\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n},\operatorname{avail}(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\},\{t\},\mathit{get\mu}_{\mathbf{G}}(t))\rangle,\circ]\text{ and }\eta(t^{\prime})\coloneq(\mathcal{B},\eta_{2}(t)+1)\\
\hfill\text{if }\eta_{1}(t)\neq\mathcal{B}\land\eta_{2}(t)<n\\
[\langle t,\operatorname{avail}(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\},\{t\},\mathit{get\mu}_{\mathbf{G}}(t))\rangle,\circ]\\
\hfill\text{if }\eta_{1}(t)=\mathcal{B}\land\eta_{2}(t)\leq n\\
\text{undefined}\\
\hfill\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{i∈I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\!:\!m_{i}.G_{i}}}\right){\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}\coloneq\begin{cases}[\langle\IntCh_{i∈I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!m_{i}.(G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}),\bigcup_{i∈I}\operatorname{avail}(\{R\},∅,G_{i})\rangle,\circ]\hskip 28.45274pt\\
\hfill\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}\land{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\\
\Mmerge\begin{cases}[\langle\ExtCh_{i\in I∧{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}∧{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}?m_{i}.(G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}),\bigcup_{i∈I}\operatorname{avail}(\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\},∅,G_{i})\rangle,\circ]\\
\Mmerge_{i\in I∧{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}∧{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{B}}\;[\langle 0,\emptyset\rangle,\ast]\\
\Mmerge_{i\in I∧{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}≠{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}∧∀t∈E.\,G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}≠[\langle t,\_\rangle,\_]}\;G_{i}{\upharpoonright}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}^{E,\mathcal{B}}\end{cases}\\
\hfill\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}\land{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\\
[\langle 0,\emptyset\rangle,\ast]\hfill\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{B}\land{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\\
\Mmerge_{i\in I}G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}\cup\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}\hfill\text{if }{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{B}\land{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\\
\end{cases}

where
\Mmerge
is defined using
\Merge
:

•

for any extended availability annotated local type

[\langle\mathit{EL},\mathit{Msg}\rangle,\circledast]
,

it holds that

[\langle\mathit{EL},\mathit{Msg}\rangle,\circledast]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise for

\circledast\in\{\ast,\circ\}
, and

•

for any two extended availability annotated local types

[\langle\mathit{EL}_{1},\mathit{Msg}_{1}\rangle,\circledast_{1}]

and

[\langle\mathit{EL}_{2},\mathit{Msg}_{2}\rangle,\circledast_{2}]
,
it holds that

[\langle\mathit{EL}_{1},\mathit{Msg}_{1}\rangle,\circledast_{1}]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.72223pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.38889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.72223pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.38889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.72223pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.38889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{5.55557pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{2.72223pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.38889pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}}[\langle\mathit{EL}_{2},\mathit{Msg}_{2}\rangle,\circledast_{2}]\coloneq[\langle(\mathit{EL}_{1}\sqcap EL_{2}),\mathit{Msg}\rangle,\circledast^{\prime}]

where

\circledast_{1},\circledast_{2},\circledast^{\prime}\in\{\ast,\circ\}
,

\circledast^{\prime}=\ast
iff
\circledast_{i}=\ast
for any
i\in\{1,2\}

and

\langle\_,\mathit{Msg}\rangle=\langle\mathit{EL}_{1},\mathit{Msg}_{1}\rangle\sqcap\langle EL_{2},\mathit{Msg}_{2}\rangle.

By assumption, every variable
t\in T
is only bound once and is bound before use in any global type.
Therefore
\eta(t)
is always defined.
The blocked projection returns an extended availability annotated local type.
Erasing the annotations yields an extended local type.

The special rule for recursion ensures that we unfold the definition (equi-recursively) if the set
\mathcal{B}
has changed since the recursion variable has been bound.
As for the standard projection, we define
\mathbf{G}{\upharpoonright}^{\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}\coloneq\mathbf{G}{\upharpoonright}^{E,\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}
.

When we want to emphasise the difference, we call projection
{\upharpoonright}_{\_}
the
standard
projection, in contrast to the
blocked
projection.
The blocked projection neglects all actions that depend on actions by any role in
\mathcal{B}
.
In the course of computation,
\mathcal{B}
grows and represents the set of roles that are not able to proceed any further since their actions depend on some previous action from a blocked role.
With each unfolding, the set either grows or there is no unfolding necessary anymore.
Therefore, it holds that
\forall n\geq\lvert\mathcal{P}\rvert-\lvert\mathcal{B}\rvert.\;\mathbf{G}{\upharpoonright}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n}^{\mathcal{B}}=\mathbf{G}{\upharpoonright}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},n+1}^{\mathcal{B}}
.
In case
n
is greater than this threshold, we omit it for readability.

By definition, the blocked projection operator mimcs the standard one for
\mathcal{B}=\emptyset
and
n=0
.

Proposition 33
.

For any global type
\mathbf{G}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
, it holds that
\mathbf{G}{\upharpoonright}^{\emptyset}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}},0}=\mathbf{G}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.

Remark 34
.

Even though the blocked projection operator and the message causality analysis both share the concept of a growing set of blocked roles, it does not seem beneficial to unify both approaches.
First, we would establish a cyclic dependency between the projection operator and the message causality analysis used when merging.
Second, the message availability analysis fixes a role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
that gets blocked.
However, the blocked merging operator would return
0
as soon as it encounters
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
again which will not yield the desired information about the channel contents.

Correspondence of Blocked Projection Operator and Blocked Languages

Lemma 35

(Correspondence of blocked projection operator and blocked languages)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a projectable global type,
G
a syntactic subterm of
\mathbf{G}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
.
Then, it holds that

\forall\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{P}.\;L^{\mathcal{B}}_{(G\ldots)}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=\mathcal{L}(G{\upharpoonright}^{\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}).

Proof.

Let
T^{\prime}
be all recursion variables in
G
that are not bound in
G
.
We define
\widehat{G}
to be the type that is obtained by substituting every
t\in T^{\prime}
by
\mathit{get\mu}_{\mathbf{G}}(t)
.
By construction,
\widehat{G}
is a global type (and not only a syntactic subterm of a latter).
Then, it suffices to show that

\forall\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{P}.\;L^{\mathcal{B}}_{(\widehat{G}\ldots)}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=\mathcal{L}(\widehat{G}{\upharpoonright}^{\mathcal{B}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}})

where
\widehat{G}
can be considered to be the overall global type for
\mathsf{GAut}(\widehat{G})
as well.
We prove this claim by induction on
\widehat{G}
.
There are two base cases.
For
0
, the claim trivially holds and
t
is no global type which
\widehat{G}
is by assumption.
For the first induction step, let
\widehat{G}=\mu t.\widehat{G^{\prime}}
and the induction hypothesis holds for
\widehat{G^{\prime}}
.
Both definition agree on the fact that
\mu t
itself does not carry any event.
The blocked projection ensures to only bind a recursion variable if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
does use it later on.
There is no such need in the blocked language along paths.
We do the same case analysis as the blocked projection operator.
First, if
t
is used after some actual event,
t
is bound and the induction hypotheses concludes the claim.
Second, if
t
is never used,
t
is not bound and the blocked language along the path yields the same language by induction hypothesis.
Third, otherwise, i.e. if the projection of the continuation yields
\langle t,\_\rangle
, the blocked language along path enters an empty loop and the claim follows.
For the second induction step, let
\widehat{G}=\Sigma_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\!:\!m_{i}.\widehat{G_{i}}
and the induction hypotheses hold for all
\widehat{G_{i}}
.
We do two case analyses and inspect the different cases in both definitions that apply: one whether
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{B}
and another one whether
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
In every but the last case, we only consider the added events and omit the recursion since this is handled by the induction hypotheses.

(i) Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
. On both sides,
\mathcal{B}
does not change in the recursive calls.
Thanks to the projection
{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
, the same event
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!m_{i}
is added before recursing.
(Due to the projection on the left side, there is no need for a case analysis for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
).

(ii) Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.

For this case, we consider each branch
i\in I
individually and do another case analysis whether
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
.

For
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
, no events are added on the left side (and branches of empty loops, yielding empty languages along the path, ignored for further projection) and the ones added on the right are projected away.

For
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
, we need another case split and check whether
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{B}
.
If so, the left side recurses and produces a language
\{\varepsilon\}
with this, the right side stops immediately and returns
\langle[0,\emptyset],\ast\rangle
which produces the same language.
If not, both sides add the same reception event for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
(and the send event is projected away).

(iii) Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{B}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
. On both sides, the result is
\{\varepsilon\}
.

(iv) Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\in\mathcal{B}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
. On both sides, no event is added but the recursions add
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
to the set
\mathcal{B}
.
These recursions are also covered by the induction hypotheses because we quantified over
\mathcal{B}
.
∎

Properties of Projectable Global Types

In this section, we establish properties which will be used in the induction step of the proof for
Lemma

27
— one for the send and one for the receive case.

Lemma 36

(Property of Projectable global types I - send)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a projectable global type and
G=\Sigma_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\!\to\!{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\!:\!m_{i}.G_{i}
be some syntactic subterm.
Then, for all
i,j\in I
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\in\mathcal{P}
, it holds that

L^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}_{(G_{i}\ldots)}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=L^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{j}\}}_{(G_{j}\ldots)}{\Downarrow}_{\Sigma_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}.

Proof.

With
Lemma

35
, it suffices to show that

G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=G_{j}{\upharpoonright}^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{j}\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

for all
i,j
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.
First, we take care of the two special cases where
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is either
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
for some
i\in I
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
, it is straightforward that
\langle[0,\emptyset],\ast\rangle
is the result of both projections.
For the second case, suppose that there was
i,j\in I
such that both projections are different and w.l.o.g.
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
.
Then, it holds that

G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=\langle[0,\emptyset],\ast\rangle
.
We know that the
\sqcap
of both projections is defined and therefore,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
cannot send as first action in the projection for
G_{j}
.
Therefore, it needs to receive.
However, then this message must be unique for this branch and by definition, the choice which branch to take is currently blocked and such a message cannot exist in the blocked projection.
Therefore, both projections are

\langle[0,\emptyset],\ast\rangle
.

For the case where
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and
\forall i.\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
, we introduce a slight variation
\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{6.04721pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.20831pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{6.04721pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.20831pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise is defined for all }i,j,\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}.

1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}}
,
then
\mathcal{B}_{i}=\mathcal{B}_{j}
.

Proof Claim I.

(Note that in the presence of empty loops, the definition prevents such kinds of merge by definition.)
Recall that
G_{i}
and
G_{j}
are two branches of the same choice in which
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is not involved.
Intuitively, this is true because the
\sqcap
-operator does not unfold recursions and a role is either agnostic to some choice (merging two recursion variables) or has learnt about the choice before encountering the recursion variable.
Towards a contradiction, suppose that there are
i,j
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
such that
\mathcal{B}_{i}\neq\mathcal{B}_{j}
.
W.l.o.g., there must be some role
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\notin\mathcal{B}_{j}
.
It follows that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\notin\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{j}\}
.
We do a case analysis whether
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
.

If so,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
receives in branch
i
and since
\sqcap
is defined, it must also receive in every continuation specified by
G_{j}
– otherwise
t
would be merged with a reception which is undefined.
(There could be subsequent branches in
G_{j}
and therefore
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
could potentially receive different messages but at least one in every continuation.)
By definition, these possible receptions must be unique and therefore distinct from
m_{i}
by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and all possible messages for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
in the continuation of
G_{j}
.
Because of this uniqueness, the send event for any reception in
G_{j}
must not be possible in
G_{i}
.
But for this, the send event must depend on the decision by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and therefore
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}
which yields a contradiction.

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}
.
By assumption,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
so there must be some reception for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}
for which the corresponding send event depends on
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\triangleright{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}!m_{i}
.
From here, the reasoning is analogous to the previous case.

End Proof of Claim I.

In Claim I,
\mathcal{B}_{i}
and
\mathcal{B}_{j}
capture all roles that depend on the choice by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
(without unfolding the recursion).
The standard projection and merge operator
\sqcap
do not unfold any recursions and
t\sqcap t
is the only rule to merge recursion variables.
Therefore, a role either has to learn about a choice by receiving some unique message before recursing or will never learn about it (since
t
maps back to the same continuation).
Recall that we omit the parameter
n
for the blocked projection operator if it is bigger than the number of iterations needed to reach a fixed point.
Combined with Claim I, it suffices to show that

G_{i}{\upharpoonright}^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{6.04721pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.20831pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{6.04721pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.20831pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise does not return undefined until the first recursion variable is encountered, for all

, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.

, and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

can be undefined.
To start with, either of both projections
{\upharpoonright}
can only return undefined when the parameter

is too small which is not the case here.
For
l\in\{i,j\}
, let

[\langle\mathit{EL}_{l},\mathit{Msg_{l}}\rangle,\circledast_{l}]=G_{l}{\upharpoonright}^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

is undefined if
\circledast_{i}\neq\circledast_{j}
or

\mathit{EL}_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}}EL_{j}
(*) is undefined.

The
first case
can only occur if

[\langle 0,\emptyset\rangle,\ast]

is merged on top level in the projection of one branch and not the other.
W.l.o.g., let
G_{i}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}^{\{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}_{i}\}}
be the branch where this happens.
On the one hand, this occurs when
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
receives from a role in
\mathcal{B}_{i}
and since
G_{i}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\sqcap G_{j}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is defined by assumption,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
also receives in the branch of
j
by definition of
\sqcap
.
For the standard merge
\sqcap
, both messages are checked to be unique for both branches.
However, if
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
can still receive the message in
G_{j}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}^{\mathcal{B}_{j}}
, this cannot depend on the choice by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
and hence must also be possible in
G_{i}
which yields a contradiction.
On the other hand,
\langle 0,\emptyset\rangle
can also be the result of projecting empty loops (after some choice operation).
Then, however, since
G_{i}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is defined, all other branches also return
\langle 0,\emptyset\rangle
for the standard projection to be defined.
In turn, since
G_{i}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\sqcap G_{j}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
,
G_{j}{\upharpoonright}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is
\langle 0,\emptyset\rangle
and hence the above projection cannot be undefined.

For the
second case
, we first observe that both
\sqcap
and
\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise agree on the type of events that can be merged, e.g., only send events can merge with send events.

mimic the ones of

In this sense, both definitions

2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{333424}{254863}{\hbox{\raisebox{6.04721pt}{$\scriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{216431}{182045}{\hbox{\raisebox{3.20831pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.43054pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}}}
and
\sqcap
agree for merging
0
, recursion variables
t
, recursion binders
\mu t.\ldots
and send events (internal choice
\IntCh
).
For reception events (external choice
\ExtCh
), they do not agree.
The variant
\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\displaystyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.29167pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise-0.86108pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\sqcap$}\crcr}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{582540}{364090}{\hbox{\raisebox{11.97223pt}{$\textstyle\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 1.80832pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise 2.58334pt\hbox{$\textstyle\sqcap$}\cr\raise
